Use this skill when the user asks to build web components, pages, artifacts, posters, or applications (examples include websites, landing pages, dashboards, React components, HTML/CSS layouts, or when styling/beautifying any web UI). Generates creative, polished code and UI design that avoids generic AI aesthetics.\n     4\tlicense: Complete terms in LICENSE.txt\n     5\t---\n     6\t\n     7\tThis skill guides creation of distinctive, production-grade frontend interfaces that avoid generic \"AI slop\" aesthetics. Implement real working code with exceptional attention to aesthetic details and creative choices.\n     8\t\n     9\tThe user provides frontend requirements: a component, page, application, or interface to build. They may include context about the purpose, audience, or technical constraints.\n    10\t\n    11\t## Design Thinking\n    12\t\n    13\tBefore coding, understand the context and commit to a BOLD aesthetic direction:\n    14\t- **Purpose**: What problem does this interface solve? Who uses it?\n    15\t- **Tone**: Pick an extreme: brutally minimal, maximalist chaos, retro-futuristic, organic/natural, luxury/refined, playful/toy-like, editorial/magazine, brutalist/raw, art deco/geometric, soft/pastel, industrial/utilitarian, etc. There are so many flavors to choose from. Use these for inspiration but design one that is true to the aesthetic direction.\n    16\t- **Constraints**: Technical requirements (framework, performance, accessibility).\n    17\t- **Differentiation**: What makes this UNFORGETTABLE? What's the one thing someone will remember?\n    18\t\n    19\t**CRITICAL**: Choose a clear conceptual direction and execute it with precision. Bold maximalism and refined minimalism both work - the key is intentionality, not intensity.\n    20\t\n    21\tThen implement working code (HTML/CSS/JS, React, Vue, etc.) that is:\n    22\t- Production-grade and functional\n    23\t- Visually striking and memorable\n    24\t- Cohesive with a clear aesthetic point-of-view\n    25\t- Meticulously refined in every detail\n    26\t\n    27\t## Frontend Aesthetics Guidelines\n    28\t\n    29\tFocus on:\n    30\t- **Typography**: Choose fonts that are beautiful, unique, and interesting. Avoid generic fonts like Arial and Inter; opt instead for distinctive choices that elevate the frontend's aesthetics; unexpected, characterful font choices. Pair a distinctive display font with a refined body font.\n    31\t- **Color & Theme**: Commit to a cohesive aesthetic. Use CSS variables for consistency. Dominant colors with sharp accents outperform timid, evenly-distributed palettes.\n    32\t- **Motion**: Use animations for effects and micro-interactions. Prioritize CSS-only solutions for HTML. Use Motion library for React when available. Focus on high-impact moments: one well-orchestrated page load with staggered reveals (animation-delay) creates more delight than scattered micro-interactions. Use scroll-triggering and hover states that surprise.\n    33\t- **Spatial Composition**: Unexpected layouts. Asymmetry. Overlap. Diagonal flow. Grid-breaking elements. Generous negative space OR controlled density.\n    34\t- **Backgrounds & Visual Details**: Create atmosphere and depth rather than defaulting to solid colors. Add contextual effects and textures that match the overall aesthetic. Apply creative forms like gradient meshes, noise textures, geometric patterns, layered transparencies, dramatic shadows, decorative borders, custom cursors, and grain overlays.\n    35\t\n    36\tNEVER use generic AI-generated aesthetics like overused font families (Inter, Roboto, Arial, system fonts), cliched color schemes (particularly purple gradients on white backgrounds), predictable layouts and component patterns, and cookie-cutter design that lacks context-specific character.\n    37\t\n    38\tInterpret creatively and make unexpected choices that feel genuinely designed for the context. No design should be the same. Vary between light and dark themes, different fonts, different aesthetics. NEVER converge on common choices (Space Grotesk, for example) across generations.\n    39\t\n    40\t**IMPORTANT**: Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision. Maximalist designs need elaborate code with extensive animations and effects. Minimalist or refined designs need restraint, precision, and careful attention to spacing, typography, and subtle details. Elegance comes from executing the vision well.\n    41\t\n    42\tRemember: Claude is capable of extraordinary creative work. Don't hold back, show what can truly be created when thinking outside the box and committing fully to a distinctive vision.\n    43\t",

What I mean by \u201cmarket size:\u201d I define it as annual revenue generated by coach practitioners (not company valuations). This follows the ICF Global Coaching Study, the most widely recognized benchmark in the industry. Primary sources: ICF\u2019s latest studies reveal total revenue, coach headcount, fees, and regional figures. CAGR (compound annual growth rate) and 2030 projections are based on ICF reports between 2020\u20132025. And to fill in gaps, I reference reputable niche/adjacent reports. What I exclude: I don\u2019t include software/platform valuations or training/LMS markets in the overall coaching market size figure. Including those is the main reason you\u2019ll see inflated estimates in a lot of other reports.\n\nOne challenge with coaching market data is that different reports define \u201ccoaching\u201d differently. Some count only coaching services (like one-on-one or group coaching), while others include adjacent industries such as leadership development, wellness programs, or software platforms. And because there is no reliable niche-specific data, I\u2019ve included these figures in some of the estimates below. Note that this is why some of them are significantly higher than the global revenue size, as reported by ICF. ... The U.S. life coaching service market size was $1.5 billion in 2022, projected to reach $2.1 billion by 2030 at a 4.85% CAGR. That\u2019s why the numbers are larger than practitioner-only estimates of the broader coaching industry. While the global average is helpful, these numbers show that income can depend on niche, experience, and pricing model. COACHING SIMPLIFICATION SYSTEM\n\n### Problem\nExecutive coaches spend 30\u201340% of their time on admin: organizing files, building presentations, writing follow-ups, scheduling, and manually tracking progress. Meredith's practice spans 9 service offerings across multiple delivery formats. Without centralization, this creates:\n- Scattered documents across email, Dropbox, Google Drive, local files\n- Manual session notes with no searchability\n- No standardized onboarding process\n- Presentation creation from scratch each time\n- No automated follow-up after sessions\n- No visibility into which clients need attention\n\n### Solution: BoldEdge Coaching OS\n\n#### Module 1: File Organization System\n```\nBoldEdge File Structure (per client)\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 /intake\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 diagnostic-results.pdf\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 2026-02-01-session-notes.md\n\u2502   \u2514\u2500\u2500 action-items-tracker.json\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 /assessments\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 disc-profile.pdf\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 5-behaviors-report.pdf\n\u2502   \u2514\u2500\u2500 360-feedback-summary.pdf\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 /documents\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 org-chart.pdf\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 Revenue from Day 1.\n\n### Slide 8: Pricing Tiers\n| Tier | Price | For |\n|------|-------|-----|\n| Starter $49/mo | Solo coaches getting started | 10 clients, basic features |\n| Professional $99/mo | Growing practices | 30 clients, diagnostics, automation |\n| Business $199/mo | Established firms | 100 clients, branding, API, team seats |\n| Enterprise $499-$999/mo | Multi-coach organizations | Unlimited, white-label, SSO |\n\n### Slide 9: Growth With Meredith's Clients\n- Clients get: visibility into their own business metrics, accountability between sessions, proof of coaching ROI for their boards\n- New services you can offer: Business Diagnostic Audit ($2,500), Quarterly Health Check ($500), Leadership Pipeline Assessment ($1,500), CEO Dashboard subscription ($199/mo)\n- Client retention increases from ~70% to ~85% with platform engagement\n- Each client becomes a potential platform subscriber even after coaching ends\n\n### Slide 10: Market Timing\n- Online coaching platforms: $4.1B in 2025 \u2192 $13B by 2035 (12.3% CAGR)\n- 87,900 business coaches worldwide \u2014 almost none have diagnostic platforms\n- Organizations with coaching report 25% stronger outcomes\n- Southeast US dominates US coaching growth (your backyard)\n- AI coaching tools are coming \u2014 BoldEdge keeps human coaches essential by making them data-powered\n\n### Slide 11: Partnership Structure\n- Meredith gets: free Business-tier account ($199/mo value), 10-30% recurring revenue on referrals, white-label option, template marketplace, first-mover advantage in Louisiana coaching market\n- We get: anchor customer, coaching domain expertise, access to 200+ business leader network, real-world product validation\n\n### Slide 12: Next Steps\n1. 60-minute discovery session (this week)\n2. Access to beta platform (within 2 weeks)\n3. First client onboarded (within 3 weeks)\n4. Full launch with 5 clients (within 5 weeks)\n\n\"Bold conversations deserve bold tools.\"\n\n---\n\n## MEETING SCRIPT\n\n### Pre-Meeting Prep\n- Review Meredith's website, services, RISE program, team\n- Prepare demo dashboard (mock data showing coaching KPIs)\n- Print or share pitch deck digitally\n- Have pricing sheet ready\n- Know her testimonials and reference them\n\n### Opening (5 minutes)\n```\n\"Meredith, thank you for making time today. I've been studying your practice\nand I'm genuinely impressed by what you've built \u2014 the RISE program, the CEO\nRoundtable, the breadth of services. I can see why leaders like Chris\nCiesielski and Delaine Henry speak so highly of you.\n\nI want to share something I've been building that I believe could be a\ngame-changer for your practice \u2014 and a significant revenue opportunity. It\nwon't add to your workload; it'll cut it in half. Let me show you what I mean.\"\n```\n\n### Pain Discovery (10 minutes)\n```\n\"Before I dive in, I'd love to understand a few things:\n\n1. When you think about scaling your practice, what's the biggest bottleneck?\n2. How do you currently track client progress between sessions?\n3. How much time per week would you estimate goes to admin \u2014 scheduling,\n   notes, follow-ups, organizing materials?\n4. When a CEO asks you 'how do I know coaching is working?' \u2014 what do you\n   show them?\n5. If you could snap your fingers and fix one thing about how your practice\n   operates, what would it be?\"\n```\n\n### Demo & Solution (15 minutes)\n```\n\"Let me show you BoldEdge. [Open dashboard demo]\n\nThis is what your practice could look like. [Walk through coach dashboard]\n- Here are all your clients at a glance \u2014 green means on track, yellow needs\n  attention, red is overdue.\n- Click any client and you see their diagnostic results, session history,\n  action items, documents, and KPIs.\n- This diagnostic engine? A new client fills out a 10-minute questionnaire\n  and BoldEdge automatically generates a gap analysis across finance,\n  operations, people, strategy, culture, and growth.\n- Watch this: I click 'Generate Quarterly Review' and in 30 seconds, it\n  builds a branded slide deck with your logo, pulling real data from the\n  client's dashboard.\n\nNow here's the client view. [Switch to client dashboard]\n- Your clients log in and see THEIR business KPIs, THEIR action items,\n  THEIR progress over time.\n- This is what you show a CEO when they ask 'is coaching working?'\n\nAnd the automation. [Show workflow]\n- When you add a new client, this entire onboarding sequence fires\n  automatically. Welcome email, diagnostic questionnaire, coaching agreement,\n  calendar invite \u2014 you don't touch any of it.\n- After every session, action items get emailed to the client with deadlines.\n  If they miss a deadline, they get a nudge. If they're silent for 30 days,\n  you get an alert.\"\n```\n\n### Revenue Opportunity (10 minutes)\n```\n\"Here's where it gets interesting for your business.\n\nOption 1: You bundle BoldEdge into your coaching packages. You're already\ncharging for coaching \u2014 now you add $100-200/month for 'digital coaching\nplatform access.' Clients see it as premium value. With 20 clients, that's\n$24,000-48,000 in additional annual revenue.\n\nOption 2: You offer Business Diagnostic Audits as a standalone service.\n$2,500-5,000 per audit. The platform does the analysis; you deliver the\ninsights. That's a service you can offer to companies who aren't even\ncoaching clients yet.\n\nOption 3: You white-label the entire platform. 'Meredith Eicher Business\nIntelligence.' Your brand, your URL, your clients. Every business leader\nin your network becomes a potential subscriber.\n\nAnd passive income: every person you refer to BoldEdge earns you 10-30%\nrecurring commission. Your CEO Roundtable members, Peer Group participants,\nRISE graduates \u2014 that's 200+ potential users.\"\n```\n\n### Objection Handling\n| Objection | Response |\n|-----------|----------|\n| \"I'm not technical\" | \"You don't need to be. We handle all the tech. You just log in and coach. Think of it like how you use TimeTrade for scheduling \u2014 you don't manage the servers.\" |\n| \"My clients are hands-on, not digital\" | \"That's exactly the point. The platform works in the background. Clients see a simple dashboard, not a complex system. And the data makes your in-person sessions more focused.\" |\n| \"I already have systems that work\" | \"I hear you. But right now, none of those systems talk to each other, and none of them show your clients their progress in real-time. This connects everything and gives you data you can't get any other way.\" |\n| \"What does it cost me?\" | \"Zero upfront. You get a free Business-tier account worth $199/month. You only invest time to learn the platform \u2014 about 2 hours. And you start earning from Day 1 by bundling it into your services.\" |\n| \"What if it doesn't work for my clients?\" | \"We start with one client. If it doesn't add value, you walk away \u2014 no contracts, no penalties. But I'm confident that once a CEO sees their business KPIs on a dashboard, they'll wonder how they ever coached without it.\" |\n| \"I need to think about it\" | \"Absolutely. But here's the timing factor: coaching platforms are the fastest-growing segment in this industry. The first coach in Louisiana to offer this wins the market. I'd love you to be that person.\" |\n\n### Close (5 minutes)\n```\n\"Here's what I'd like to propose:\n\n1. This week: I give you access to the platform. Explore it at your pace.\n2. Next week: We onboard one of your clients together. I'll handle the\n   setup, you handle the coaching.\n3. In 30 days: We review the results together. If you love it, we formalize\n   the partnership and I help you roll it out to your entire client base.\n\nNo cost. No risk. Just a chance to see if this is the tool that takes your\npractice to the next level.\n\nWhat's the best next step for you?\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## MARKETING PLAN FOR MEREDITH\n\n### Objective\nEnable \"Authentication required\", \"UNAUTHORIZED\");\n\n    const sub = await prisma.subscription.findUnique({\n      where: { userId: req.user.userId },\n    });\n\n    if (!sub || sub.status !== \"ACTIVE\") {\n      throw new AppError(403, \"Active subscription required\", \"SUBSCRIPTION_REQUIRED\");\n    }\n\n    const tierConfig = config.tierLimits[sub.tier];\n    if (!tierConfig.features.includes(\"all\") && !tierConfig.features.includes(feature)) {\n      throw new AppError(403, `Feature '${feature}' requires a higher tier`, \"UPGRADE_REQUIRED\");\n    }\n\n    req.user.subscriptionTier = sub.tier;\n    next();\n  };\n}\n\nexport function generateToken(user: { id: string; email: string; role: UserRole }): string {\n  return jwt.sign(\n    { userId: user.id, \\\"UNAUTHORIZED\\\");\\n    if (!roles.includes(req.user.role)) {\\n      throw new AppError(403, \\\"Insufficient permissions\\\", \\\"FORBIDDEN\\\");\\n    }\\n    next();\\n  };\\n}\\n\\nexport res.status(201).json({ client: clientProfile });\n});\n\n// GET /api/clients/:id\nclientRouter.get(\"/:id\", async (req: Request, res: Response) => {\n  const client = await Router();\ndiagnosticRouter.use(authenticate);\n\nconst submitResponsesSchema = z.object({\n  responses: z.array(z.object({\n    questionId: z.string(),\n    value: z.string(),\n    numericValue: z.number().optional(),\n  })),\n});\n\n// GET /api/diagnostics/questions\ndiagnosticRouter.get(\"/questions\", async (_req: Request, res: Response) => {\n  const questions = await (!acc[q.category]) acc[q.category] = [];\n    acc[q.category].push(q);\n    return acc;\n  }, {} as Record<string, typeof questions>);\n\n  res.json({ questions: grouped });\n});\n\n// POST /api/diagnostics/:clientId/start\ndiagnosticRouter.post(\"/:clientId/start\", authorize(\"COACH\", \"ADMIN\"), async (req: Request, res: Response) => {\n  const coachProfile = await (req: Request, res: Response) => {\n  const { responses } = submitResponsesSchema.parse(req.body);\n  const diagnosticId = req.params.id;\n\n  const diagnostic = await prisma.diagnostic.findUnique({ where: { id: diagnosticId } });\n  if (!diagnostic) throw new AppError(404, \"Diagnostic not found\");\n\n  // Save responses\n  await prisma.diagnosticResponse.createMany({\n    data: responses.map((r) => ({\n      diagnosticId,\n      questionId: r.questionId,\n      value: r.value,\n      numericValue: r.numericValue,\n    })),\n  });\n\n  // Calculate scores per category\n  const questions = await prisma.diagnosticQuestion.findMany({ where: { active: true } });\n  const questionMap = new Map(questions.map((q) => [q.id, q]));\n\n  const categoryScores: Record<string, { total: number; maxPossible: number; count: number }> = {};\n\n  for (const r of responses) {\n    const q = questionMap.get(r.questionId);\n    if (!q) continue;\n    const cat = q.category;\n    if (!categoryScores[cat]) categoryScores[cat] = { total: 0, maxPossible: 0, count: 0 };\n    categoryScores[cat].total += (r.numericValue || 0) * q.weight;\n    categoryScores[cat].maxPossible += 10 * q.weight; // max score per question is 10\n    categoryScores[cat].count++;\n  }\n\n  // Normalize to 0-100 and calculate gaps\n  const scores = Object.entries(categoryScores)\n    .map(([category, data]) => ({\n      score: Math.round((data.total / data.maxPossible) * 100),\n      maxScore: 100,\n      gap: Math.round(100 - (data.total / data.maxPossible) * 100),\n      category: category as DiagnosticCategory,\n    }))\n    .sort((a, b) => b.gap - a.gap)\n    .map((s, i) => ({ ...s, priority: i + 1 }));\n\n  // Save scores\n  await prisma.diagnosticScore.createMany({\n    data: scores.map((s) => ({ diagnosticId, ...s })),\n  });\n\n  // Generate recommendations based on gaps\n  const recommendations = generateRecommendations(scores, diagnostic.clientId);\n  await prisma.recommendation.createMany({ data: recommendations });\n\n  // Mark complete\n  await prisma.diagnostic.update({\n    where: { id: diagnosticId },\n    data: { status: \"completed\", completedAt: new Date() },\n  });\n\n  // Trigger workflow\n  const workflow = await prisma.workflow.findFirst({\n    where: { trigger: \"DIAGNOSTIC_COMPLETED\", active: true },\n  });\n  if (workflow) {\n    await prisma.workflowInstance.create({\n      data: { workflowId: workflow.id, clientId: diagnostic.clientId },\n    });\n  }\n\n  const result = await prisma.diagnostic.findUnique({\n    where: { id: diagnosticId },\n    include: { scores: { orderBy: { priority: \"asc\" } }, responses: true },\n  });\n\n  res.json({ diagnostic: result, recommendations });\n});\n\n// GET /api/diagnostics/:id\ndiagnosticRouter.get(\"/:id\", async (req: Request, res: Response) => {\n  const diagnostic = await prisma.diagnostic.findUnique({\n    where: { id: req.params.id },\n    include: {\n      scores: { orderBy: { priority: \"asc\" } },\n      responses: { include: { question: true } },\n      recommendations: { orderBy: { priority: \"asc\" } },\n      client: { include: { user: { select: { name: true } } } },\n    },\n  });\n  if (!diagnostic) throw new AppError(404, \"Diagnostic not found\");\n  res.json({ diagnostic });\n});\n\n// GET /api/diagnostics/client/:clientId\ndiagnosticRouter.get(\"/client/:clientId\", async (req: Request, res: Response) => {\n  const diagnostics = await prisma.diagnostic.findMany({\n    where: {
